Back home in Okanogan County, she threw herself into eco-fashion projects and online art communities, many of which emphasized minimalism and \"clean eating\" aesthetics that made her increasingly critical of her own body when she compared it to stylized images of other textile artists. During a particularly stressful period at the shop—juggling a rush of wedding garments, vendor disputes, and late-night dye experiments—she began coping by eating large, improvised meals alone in her small kitchen after work, then feeling panicked about the impact on her body and taking drastic measures to counteract it. Over six months, this cycle escalated, she withdrew from hikes and yoga classes out of embarrassment about how she looked, and a close friend from her local art center finally urged her to seek psychiatric help after Daphne confided that her eating and efforts to control her weight felt \"out of control\" and were crowding out her art and sleep.\n- Chief Complaint: “Once I start eating in bulge in a photo, I panic and start thinking, ‘I’m going to blow up and look huge in my clothes,’ and it feels like my whole day is ruined until I do something to get the weight back down.\n- S046 (Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance): Most days I decide if I’m worth anything by how flat my stomach looks in the mirror before my shower, and if I see any roll or softness I just feel like a (Eating_Large_Amount): On those nights after the shop, I can stand at my tiny kitchen counter and go through half a loaf of bread with peanut butter, a frozen pizza, leftover takeout, and whatever snacks are around in less than an hour, even when I wasn’t that hungry to start with.\n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start in on the evening eating, it’s like a switch flips and I’m just shoveling For at least the last few months, most weeks I have several nights where I gorge myself after work and then either make myself throw up or punish myself with fasting or long walks to try to undo it.\n- functional_impairment: My nights are so wrapped up in binging, purging, and planning how to make up for it that I’ve stopped going to hikes and art meetups, and I’m often exhausted and distracted at the shop the next day, making more mistakes with customers’ clothes.\n- duration: This whole pattern of losing control with food at night and then doing drastic things to counter it has To be honest, I try to avoid looking at the scale entirely because seeing the numbers just causes me too much panic and distress.\n\nDoctor: Could you describe what and how much food you typically eat during one of these evening episodes where you feel you've lost control?\n\nPatient: I usually just stand at my kitchen counter and eat a massive amount of food in less than an hour, even if I wasn't even hungry. I'll go through half a loaf of bread with peanut butter, a frozen pizza, leftover takeout, and whatever snacks I can find. It's so humiliating because I just can't seem to stop myself once I start.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't know the exact numbers, would you say your current weight is generally considered underweight, normal weight, or overweight, and have you recently lost a large amount of weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what category my weight falls into, or if I've lost a large amount of weight recently. I just know that I am constantly terrified of gaining anything, and when I look in the mirror, I feel absolutely huge.\n\nDoctor: Have family, friends, or doctors ever expressed concern that you are too thin or underweight, even if you feel otherwise?\n\nPatient: No, nobody has ever expressed concern that I'm too thin or underweight. A close friend of mine was really worried, but that was because she knew my eating habits were getting completely out of control, not because of my weight."
